- **bussin-drizzle**: House rules for Drizzle ORM in TypeScript - schemas inside each feature, no cross-feature foreign keys, no global schema or db.query, a migration with every schema change. - **bussin-honest-feedback**: Give a direct, honest assessment, not an agreeable one: verdict first, a rating out of 10, assumptions marked verified, plausible or weak, 'you're wrong' when the user is, and 'I don't know' when uncertain. Use when the user asks to rate, critique, sanity-check, poke holes in or stress-test an idea, plan, design or decision, says 'be honest', 'don't sugarcoat' or 'am I wrong', or wants a second opinion. - **bussin-react**: House rules for React 19 and Next.js App Router code - server components first with small client islands, shadcn/ui owned in src/shared/ui, reuse before building, compound components by default so components extend without breaking callers, TanStack Query for client-side server state, forms that reuse the feature's zod DTOs, and fast, secure UI. - **bust-ai-hallucinations**: AI generates an import for a package that sounds real but has never existed on PyPI, npm, crates.io, or pkg.go.dev. ([404-not-found/codebusters](https://github.com/404-not-found/codebusters/tree/HEAD/skills/bust-ai-hallucinations))
- **bust-apologizer**: Use when the user complains that Claude's previous answer was wrong, low quality, ignored instructions, broke a workflow, repeated a mistake, wasted time, or failed to learn from feedback. Applies a severity-aware apology style: acknowledge the miss, classify severity, show a small cheeky ASCII remorse meter, explain the correction plan, and avoid over-apologizing. ([paperli/bust-apologizer](https://github.com/paperli/bust-apologizer/tree/HEAD/skills/bust-apologizer) · ★1)
- **bust-async-without-await**: `async` functions that never actually `await` anything — synchronous code dressed up in async syntax. The function runs synchronously, blocks the event loop on any slow operation, and gives callers a false sense that it's non-blocking. This is one of the most common AI code generation mistakes. ([404-not-found/codebusters](https://github.com/404-not-found/codebusters/tree/HEAD/skills/bust-async-without-await))
- **bust-blocking-calls**: Synchronous blocking calls inside async contexts — reading files, making HTTP requests, or querying databases with sync APIs inside `async def` functions, `async` event handlers, or event loops — stalling the entire event loop. ([404-not-found/codebusters](https://github.com/404-not-found/codebusters/tree/HEAD/skills/bust-blocking-calls))
- **bust-ci-cd-gaps**: Missing, broken, or toothless steps in the CI/CD pipeline — lint gates that don't enforce, test jobs with `continue-on-error`, missing security scans, and deploy pipelines that can push broken code to production. ([404-not-found/codebusters](https://github.com/404-not-found/codebusters/tree/HEAD/skills/bust-ci-cd-gaps))
- **bust-circular-deps**: Circular dependency chains — where module A imports B, B imports C, and C imports A — causing import errors, initialization failures, and architectures that cannot be partially loaded or tested. ([404-not-found/codebusters](https://github.com/404-not-found/codebusters/tree/HEAD/skills/bust-circular-deps))
- **bust-convention-drift**: A second way of doing something that already has an established pattern in the codebase — two HTTP clients, two error handling strategies, two auth patterns, two ways to write database queries — introduced by AI or a new contributor who didn't check how it was done before. ([404-not-found/codebusters](https://github.com/404-not-found/codebusters/tree/HEAD/skills/bust-convention-drift))
- **bust-dead-code**: Unused imports, unreachable branches, zombie functions that are never called, commented-out code blocks, and feature flags that have been fully rolled out but never cleaned up. ([404-not-found/codebusters](https://github.com/404-not-found/codebusters/tree/HEAD/skills/bust-dead-code))
- **bust-deploy-risk**: Deployment practices that make production releases dangerous — no rollback plan, untested migrations, missing feature flags, and deploying directly to production without validation. ([404-not-found/codebusters](https://github.com/404-not-found/codebusters/tree/HEAD/skills/bust-deploy-risk))
- **bust-env-parity**: "Works on my machine" — mismatches between development, CI, and production environments caused by missing `.env.example`, unpinned tool versions, inconsistent OS behaviour, missing seed data, and dev-only dependencies leaking into production. ([404-not-found/codebusters](https://github.com/404-not-found/codebusters/tree/HEAD/skills/bust-env-parity))
- **bust-exposed-internals**: Sensitive data leaking through error messages, logs, API responses, or debug output — stack traces returned to users, PII in application logs, internal system paths in error responses, and database schemas leaked through verbose errors. ([404-not-found/codebusters](https://github.com/404-not-found/codebusters/tree/HEAD/skills/bust-exposed-internals))
- **bust-flaky-tests**: Tests that pass and fail non-deterministically — depending on execution order, timing, shared state, external services, or random data — causing developers to re-run CI until they get green, and eroding trust in the entire test suite. ([404-not-found/codebusters](https://github.com/404-not-found/codebusters/tree/HEAD/skills/bust-flaky-tests))
- **bust-formatting**: Code that violates language-specific style conventions — inconsistent indentation, mixed quote styles, wrong line lengths, and formatting that would be caught automatically by a linter or formatter. ([404-not-found/codebusters](https://github.com/404-not-found/codebusters/tree/HEAD/skills/bust-formatting))
- **bust-framework-antipatterns**: Code that fights the framework instead of working with it — reimplementing what the framework already provides, bypassing its conventions, or using its primitives in ways they were not designed for. ([404-not-found/codebusters](https://github.com/404-not-found/codebusters/tree/HEAD/skills/bust-framework-antipatterns))
- **bust-god-objects**: Classes or modules that know too much, do too much, and own too much — making the codebase impossible to test, extend, or understand. ([404-not-found/codebusters](https://github.com/404-not-found/codebusters/tree/HEAD/skills/bust-god-objects))
- **bust-hardcoded-secrets**: API keys, passwords, tokens, private keys, and other credentials baked directly into source code — where they will be committed to version control and potentially exposed to anyone with repository access. ([404-not-found/codebusters](https://github.com/404-not-found/codebusters/tree/HEAD/skills/bust-hardcoded-secrets))
- **bust-incomplete-refactor**: **Bad:** ```python # user_service.py — AI renamed this def get_user_by_id(user_id: int) -&gt; User: # was: fetch_user() return db.query(User).get(user_id) ([404-not-found/codebusters](https://github.com/404-not-found/codebusters/tree/HEAD/skills/bust-incomplete-refactor))
- **bust-injection**: SQL injection, command injection, template injection, LDAP injection, and other injection vulnerabilities — where user-controlled input is concatenated into a query, command, or template that is then executed. ([404-not-found/codebusters](https://github.com/404-not-found/codebusters/tree/HEAD/skills/bust-injection))
- **bust-insecure-defaults**: Security vulnerabilities introduced by insecure default configurations — open CORS policies, weak cryptography settings, missing authentication, disabled TLS verification, and other cases where the "easy" setup is also the insecure one. ([404-not-found/codebusters](https://github.com/404-not-found/codebusters/tree/HEAD/skills/bust-insecure-defaults))
- **bust-legacy-patterns**: Code that uses outdated language patterns — callbacks where async/await is available, `var` instead of `const`/`let`, string concatenation instead of template literals, and other patterns superseded by modern equivalents that are cleaner and safer. ([404-not-found/codebusters](https://github.com/404-not-found/codebusters/tree/HEAD/skills/bust-legacy-patterns))
- **bust-logic-errors**: Incorrect logic that passes tests and linting but produces wrong results — off-by-one errors, inverted conditions, wrong operators (`&&` vs `||`, `&gt;` vs `&gt;=`), incorrect boundary handling, and silent integer/float precision issues. The #1 category of AI-generated code failure. ([404-not-found/codebusters](https://github.com/404-not-found/codebusters/tree/HEAD/skills/bust-logic-errors))
- **bust-memory-leaks**: Event listeners not removed, timers not cleared, closures holding references to large objects, unclosed file handles or database connections, and other patterns that prevent the garbage collector from reclaiming memory. ([404-not-found/codebusters](https://github.com/404-not-found/codebusters/tree/HEAD/skills/bust-memory-leaks))
- **bust-missing-auth-check**: API endpoints that authenticate the caller (verify *who* they are) but forget to authorise the action (verify *whether they're allowed to do this specific thing*). A logged-in user can read, modify, or delete another user's data by simply changing an ID in the request. AI agents reliably generate authentication middleware but almost never add per-resource ownership checks. ([404-not-found/codebusters](https://github.com/404-not-found/codebusters/tree/HEAD/skills/bust-missing-auth-check))
- **bust-missing-db-index**: Database queries on unindexed columns — causing full table scans that are fast in development (100 rows) and catastrophically slow in production (1M rows). AI agents generate queries without knowing the data volume or access patterns, and almost never add indexes. ([404-not-found/codebusters](https://github.com/404-not-found/codebusters/tree/HEAD/skills/bust-missing-db-index))
- **bust-missing-input-validation**: API endpoints, functions, and handlers that trust all incoming data without validating it — accepting malformed types, out-of-range values, oversized payloads, or missing required fields. AI agents commonly skip validation because the happy-path logic works on the sample data they're given. ([404-not-found/codebusters](https://github.com/404-not-found/codebusters/tree/HEAD/skills/bust-missing-input-validation))
- **bust-missing-observability**: Production code paths with no logging, no metrics, and no alerting — where failures are invisible until a user reports them, where you can't answer "is this working right now?", and where diagnosing an incident requires guesswork instead of data. ([404-not-found/codebusters](https://github.com/404-not-found/codebusters/tree/HEAD/skills/bust-missing-observability))
- **bust-missing-pagination**: Queries, APIs, and data fetches that return unbounded result sets — no `LIMIT`, no cursor, no page size — causing out-of-memory crashes, timeouts, and cascading failures as the dataset grows. ([404-not-found/codebusters](https://github.com/404-not-found/codebusters/tree/HEAD/skills/bust-missing-pagination))
- **bust-missing-tests**: Critical code paths with no test coverage — business logic that runs without tests, edge cases that have never been verified, and new features shipped without any corresponding tests. ([404-not-found/codebusters](https://github.com/404-not-found/codebusters/tree/HEAD/skills/bust-missing-tests))
- **bust-missing-timeouts**: **Bad:** ```python import requests ([404-not-found/codebusters](https://github.com/404-not-found/codebusters/tree/HEAD/skills/bust-missing-timeouts))
- **bust-missing-transactions**: **Bad:** ```python def transfer_funds(from_id: int, to_id: int, amount: Decimal): sender = Account.objects.get(id=from_id) sender.balance -= amount sender.save() # Committed immediately ([404-not-found/codebusters](https://github.com/404-not-found/codebusters/tree/HEAD/skills/bust-missing-transactions))
- **bust-mutable-defaults**: Mutable default arguments and shared mutable state that is accidentally shared across all callers — Python's most notorious footgun, and a pattern AI agents reproduce constantly because the code looks correct and passes basic tests. The bug only appears when the function is called more than once. ([404-not-found/codebusters](https://github.com/404-not-found/codebusters/tree/HEAD/skills/bust-mutable-defaults))
- **bust-n-plus-one**: N+1 query patterns — where code executes one query to fetch a list of records and then one additional query per record to fetch related data — causing performance that degrades linearly with the number of records. ([404-not-found/codebusters](https://github.com/404-not-found/codebusters/tree/HEAD/skills/bust-n-plus-one))
- **bust-naming**: Misleading, abbreviated, cryptic, or non-idiomatic names for variables, functions, classes, and modules — making code harder to read than it needs to be and hiding intent. ([404-not-found/codebusters](https://github.com/404-not-found/codebusters/tree/HEAD/skills/bust-naming))
- **bust-orm-abuse**: Misuse of Object-Relational Mappers — both using an ORM where raw SQL would be more appropriate, and writing raw SQL where the ORM would be cleaner and safer. ([404-not-found/codebusters](https://github.com/404-not-found/codebusters/tree/HEAD/skills/bust-orm-abuse))
- **bust-outdated-deps**: Dependencies that are multiple major versions behind, have known CVEs, have been abandoned by their maintainers, or create transitive vulnerabilities that put the application at risk. ([404-not-found/codebusters](https://github.com/404-not-found/codebusters/tree/HEAD/skills/bust-outdated-deps))
- **bust-over-engineering**: Solutions that are more complex than the problem requires — abstractions built for hypothetical futures, infrastructure sized for 100x the current load, and patterns applied because they sound impressive rather than because they solve a real problem. ([404-not-found/codebusters](https://github.com/404-not-found/codebusters/tree/HEAD/skills/bust-over-engineering))
- **bust-pr-hygiene**: Pull requests that are too large to review, lack context, bundle unrelated changes, or sit open so long they become merge conflict nightmares — all of which lead to superficial reviews and bugs that slip through. ([404-not-found/codebusters](https://github.com/404-not-found/codebusters/tree/HEAD/skills/bust-pr-hygiene))
- **bust-race-conditions**: Check-then-act patterns, non-atomic counter increments, double-submit vulnerabilities, and shared mutable state accessed concurrently — where the gap between "check" and "act" allows another thread or request to invalidate the assumption. ([404-not-found/codebusters](https://github.com/404-not-found/codebusters/tree/HEAD/skills/bust-race-conditions))
- **bust-stub-code**: Placeholder, scaffold, or TODO code that was written as a temporary skeleton and accidentally shipped — `throw new Error("not implemented")`, hardcoded return values, `pass` bodies in production paths, and `// TODO: implement` comments in code that is actively called. ([404-not-found/codebusters](https://github.com/404-not-found/codebusters/tree/HEAD/skills/bust-stub-code))
- **bust-swallowed-errors**: Exceptions and errors that are silently caught and discarded — making failures invisible, turning bugs into mystery behaviour, and removing any chance of diagnosis or recovery. ([404-not-found/codebusters](https://github.com/404-not-found/codebusters/tree/HEAD/skills/bust-swallowed-errors))
- **bust-tech-debt-hotspots**: Files and modules that are simultaneously high-churn (changed frequently) and low-quality (low test coverage, high complexity, many bugs) — the areas of the codebase where every change is risky and takes longer than it should. ([404-not-found/codebusters](https://github.com/404-not-found/codebusters/tree/HEAD/skills/bust-tech-debt-hotspots))
- **bust-test-quality**: Two related problems that together make tests useless: tests that exist but verify the wrong things (asserting implementation instead of behaviour), and code that is structurally impossible to test (hidden dependencies, logic buried in I/O, non-determinism). Both produce the same outcome — a green CI that misses real bugs. ([404-not-found/codebusters](https://github.com/404-not-found/codebusters/tree/HEAD/skills/bust-test-quality))
- **bust-tight-coupling**: Components that are so tightly bound to their dependencies that they cannot be tested in isolation, swapped for a different implementation, or reused in a different context. ([404-not-found/codebusters](https://github.com/404-not-found/codebusters/tree/HEAD/skills/bust-tight-coupling))
- **bust-type-safety**: Type system holes that disable static analysis and allow runtime type errors to slip through — TypeScript `any`, unchecked casts, missing null guards on external data, and Python code that ignores type hints at the boundaries where data enters the system. ([404-not-found/codebusters](https://github.com/404-not-found/codebusters/tree/HEAD/skills/bust-type-safety))
- **bust-unnecessary-recompute**: Expensive computations that are re-run on every render, every request, or every event — when the input hasn't changed and the result could be cached or memoized. ([404-not-found/codebusters](https://github.com/404-not-found/codebusters/tree/HEAD/skills/bust-unnecessary-recompute))
- **bust-wrong-architecture**: Code structured around an architectural pattern that doesn't fit the problem — making the system harder to understand, extend, and maintain than it needs to be. ([404-not-found/codebusters](https://github.com/404-not-found/codebusters/tree/HEAD/skills/bust-wrong-architecture))
- **bust-wrong-framework**: Using a framework that doesn't match the problem — either too heavy, too light, or simply the wrong tool — leading to unnecessary complexity, fighting the framework, or missing built-in capabilities. ([404-not-found/codebusters](https://github.com/404-not-found/codebusters/tree/HEAD/skills/bust-wrong-framework))
- **bust-wrong-workflow**: Development workflow practices that slow teams down, introduce risk, or make it impossible to ship safely — including branching strategies that don't match team size, missing CI/CD gates, and deploy practices that have no rollback path. ([404-not-found/codebusters](https://github.com/404-not-found/codebusters/tree/HEAD/skills/bust-wrong-workflow))

([gabrielepiaggesi/busta-paga-2.0](https://github.com/gabrielepiaggesi/busta-paga-2.0/tree/HEAD/plugins/busta-paga-2-0/skills/busta-paga-2-0))
- **busta-paga-leggi**: Read Italian payslips line by line with net-pay math. Use when asked busta paga, payslip Italy, read payslip, netto in busta. ([terzastella/italy-work-skills](https://github.com/terzastella/italy-work-skills/tree/HEAD/skills/busta-paga-leggi) · ★2)
- **buster-call**: Buster Call — quality and security veto. Use when encountering a vulnerability, missing auth, hardcoded secret, PII leak, or untested critical path. Logs to brain/vivre-cards.md.
